Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). train.log] |\\\\n| Epoch 3 eval_loss | 0.36 | **0.49 (收敛了!)** | [无 train.log] |\\\\n| 平均 train_loss | 1.26 | 12.74 | [无数据] |\\\\n\\\\n**重要发现: v2 虽然起步 loss=48，但最终 eval_loss 收敛到 0.49，模型实际上学到了。**\\\\n\\\\n### class_weights 代码分析\\\\nclass_weights 在 WeightedTrainer.compute_loss() 中正确实现：\\\\n- sample_idx 由 ImplicitSFTDataset.__getitem__() 返回 (line 85)\\\\n- PaddingCollator.__call__() 正确传递 (line 128-136)\\\\n- compute_loss() 中 `inputs.pop(\\\\\\\"sample_idx\\\\\\\")` 获取，条件 `self._class_weights is not None and sample_indices is not None` 控制 (line 227)\\\\n- v2 (class_weights=\\\\\\\"auto\\\\\\\"): 权重 internal=0.743, det=1.527，正确计算并应用\\\\n- v3 (class_weights=null): 无权重，`loss = per_sample_loss.mean()`\\\\n\\\\n**class_weights 代码本身没有 bug。v2 和 v3 的 step 10 loss 一致（48.15），证明 class_weights 不是 loss 差异的主因。** class_weights 只是对 per-sample loss 的乘法缩放（0.74x~1.53x），不会改变 loss 的数量级。\\\\n\\\\n### plan-002-formal-v6 数据路径\\\\n- 数据来源: `results/plan_c/` 目录（11 个 task_type 子目录的 labeled_steps.jsonl）\\\\n- 加载: 2040 samples from 11 files\\\\n- 标签分布: deterministic 1830 (89.7%), internal 210 (10.3%)\\\\n- 与 ablation-b 的 implicit 数据是**同源数据，但格式不同**（explicit router 用 observation+action_history+label 格式，implicit 用 messages chat 格式）\\\\n- **plan-002-formal-v6 也退化了**: 全预测 deterministic (internal: 0/30, deterministic: 390/390)\\\\n\\\\n### 结论：根因判定\\\\n\\\\n**\\\\\\\"数据文件不同\\\\\\\"假说被证伪（对 v3）**：v3 使用和 ablation-b 完全相同的数据（MD5 一致），但仍然异常。\\\\n\\\\n**真正的变量是训练策略的组合**：\\\\n1. **`upsample_minority: true`**（ablation-b 独有）：在 89.7% deterministic 的极端不平衡数据上，WeightedRandomSampler 确保 internal 样本被充分采样。没有它，模型每 batch 几乎只看到 deterministic 样本。\\\\n2. **`gradient_accumulation_steps: 1 vs 8`**：有效 batch 4 vs 32。较大 batch + 无上采样 = 每步看到的 internal 样本更少。\\\\n\\\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\\\n\\\\n**建议下一步**：\\\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, \"notes\": \"Generation-based routing eval 推翻了此前 logit-level 的 success 结论。\\n\\n**Logit-level (Trainer eval, 训练时)**: accuracy=74.5%, balanced_acc=79.1%, internal_recall=84.9% — 看起来成功。\\n**Generation-based eval**: accuracy=53.3%, balanced_acc=41.5%, internal_recall=26.7% — 实际失败。\\n\\ninternal_recall=26.7% < 30% 判定线 → conclusion 改为 negative。\\n\\n三种 thinking 模式（thinking_on, thinking_off, thinking_on+strip）结果完全一致，说明 thinking mode 不是根因。\\n\\n与 ablation-b 对比: accuracy 53.3% vs 65.4%, balanced_acc 41.5% vs 59.3%, internal_recall 26.7% vs 51.5%。全面落后。\\n\\n与 plan-002-generative-v1 同样的问题模式：logit-level 指标与 generation-level 指标严重不一致。模型在 next-token prediction (loss) 层面学会了区分路由，但在自回归生成时无法可靠地输出 [SIMULATE] 前缀。\\n\\n根因推测：causal LM 的 generation 和 teacher-forcing eval 是不同的推理模式。训练时看到完整序列可以区分路由，但生成时第一个 token 的选择（是否输出 \\\"[\\\" 开始 [SIMULATE]）不够确定。\"}, \"plan-003-baseline-245eps\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-14T08:11:02.369Z\", 的极端不平衡数据上，WeightedRandomSampler 确保 internal 样本被充分采样。没有它，模型每 batch 几乎只看到 deterministic 样本。\\n2. **`gradient_accumulation_steps: 1 vs 8`**：有效 batch 4 vs 32。较大 batch + 无上采样 = 每步看到的 internal 样本更少。\\n\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\n\\n**建议下一步**：\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_12/projects/simgate-modular-routing/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — simgate-modular-routing\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR short paper, 4 pages + references)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026 (11 days remaining)\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nLLM agents on rule-governed environments face catastrophic failures from hallucinated state transitions. Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates these failures — but what exactly does routing learn? We introduce episode-level counterfactual analysis (per-step oracle labeling degenerates to 97.4% ties in sparse-reward settings) and discover that **death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal** on ScienceWorld (Δ_full=+29.60 [A], Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], DAF=90.41% [A]; Pearson r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A] across 11 task types / 245 episodes [A]; source: plan-012-full). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd/Δ_full=9.6% [A]). Key exception: find-non-living-thing (n=40, zero deaths, Δ_full=+9.58, all non-death performance difference) [A]. The contribution is the empirical finding (death avoidance dominance) + the diagnostic methodology (episode-level counterfactual labeling), not the router architecture itself.\\n\\n## Core Claims (2 claims for short paper)\\n\\n**C1 (Death avoidance dominates routing signal)**: On ScienceWorld, deterministic simulation dramatically outperforms LLM reasoning (Δ_full=+29.60 [A]), with **death avoidance as the dominant mechanism** (90.41% of the performance gap [A], measured by Δ decomposition; plan-012-full, 11 tasks / 245 eps [A]). Episode-level label distribution: det-preferred 86 / internal-preferred 9 / no-preference 150 (out of 245 episodes) [A]. Death rates: internal 42.9% / deterministic 16.3% [A]. Non-death score deltas are small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). Key exception: find-non-living-thing (n=40, zero deaths, Δ_full=+9.58, all non-death performance difference) [A]. Pearson r=0.9698 (p=8.12e-07) [A] between per-task death rate difference and Δ_full across 11 task types [A]. The value of deterministic computation in interactive environments is concentrated in catastrophic failure prevention, not positive task guidance.\\n\\n**C2 (Episode-level counterfactual labeling as diagnostic methodology)**: Per-step oracle labeling degenerates in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld). Episode-level counterfactual analysis — running complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies — overcomes this circularity and reveals the death-avoidance mechanism. The resulting labels train a lightweight router (Qwen3-1.7B) that serves as a **diagnostic probe** for LLM failure modes. [Hypothesis, pending plan-005]: the router may learn within-task-type early warning signals of catastrophic failure from early observations, beyond simple task-type classification.\\n\\n**D013 note**: C1 no longer claims \\\"selective > unconditional.\\\" Router training (plan-002/003) proceeds — if router ≈ always-det, that IS the finding; comparison with rule-based baseline (plan-011) quantifies whether learned routing adds value over keyword heuristics.\\n\\n## Positioning vs Tool-Use Literature (Dream-driven addition)\\n\\n**Critical gap identified by all 3 Dream tiers**: The core pipeline (counterfactual labels → SFT small classifier → selective invocation) is standard in tool-use learning. Must position against:\\n- **Toolformer** (Schick et al. 2024): Self-supervised tool-use learning via API call insertion. SimGate differs: hindsight labels from environment forking (not self-supervised), safety-aware framing (not general tool selection).\\n- **ART** (Paranjape et al. 2023): Automated tool selection via LLM reasoning. SimGate: dedicated lightweight router, not LLM-as-router.\\n- **Chameleon** (Lu et al. 2024): LLM-as-planner routing to diverse tools. SimGate: single modality (deterministic computation), explicit small router.\\n- **ToolkenGPT** (Hao et al. 2024): Token-level tool triggers. SimGate: step-level routing with counterfactual labels.\\n\\n**Our differentiation**: (1) Episode-level counterfactual labeling — overcomes per-step oracle circularity in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate), providing ground-truth episode outcomes rather than tie-break artifacts. (2) Empirical finding: death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal [A] — quantifies WHERE and WHY tool invocation helps (catastrophic failure prevention, not positive guidance). (3) Diagnostic framework — counterfactual analysis + router as probe reveals systematic LLM failure modes across task categories. (4) The contribution is the finding + methodology, not the router architecture — this distinguishes from \\\"we built a better tool selector\\\" papers.\\n\\n**Paper must include a dedicated Related Work section** (not just intro paragraphs). Minimum coverage: tool-use learning (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon), adaptive computation (CogRouter, ARES, Mixture-of-Depths), world model routing (ITP-R, Dyna-Think), agent frameworks (ReAct).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: LLM agents hallucinate state transitions on rule-governed environments (temperature physics, chemical reactions), leading to catastrophic failures. ITP-R routes to an approximate LLM world model but underperforms pure internal reasoning (62.58 vs CogRouter 84.6 on ScienceWorld). ReTool/AgentMath learn when to invoke code interpreters but use implicit routing entangled with the backbone. Tool-use learning methods (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon) learn general tool selection but don't specialize for catastrophic failure prevention or use counterfactual trajectory labeling.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We use episode-level counterfactual analysis to understand WHAT routing learns. Per-step oracle labeling degenerates (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld), so we run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode-level outcomes. Key finding: ~90% of routing signal is death avoidance [A] — deterministic computation's value is almost entirely concentrated in preventing catastrophic failures (-100 penalties). ScienceWorld actions use generic interaction verbs (connect/move/pick up), not science-specific keywords, so no keyword li\\n\\n... 若 internal-preferred ≥ 3/25（≥12%）→ 保持现有 framing，plan-002 router SFT 使用 class-weighted\\\\n    loss 应对不平衡\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n    不选的方案：(b) 人为增加 internal episodes → p-hacking 风险；(c) 加 MATH track → 12 天 DDL 下风险太大\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n    另外 Reviewer 指出 genuine-det 分类中 find-living-thing（+0.25）和 freeze（+2.0）在 n=5 下无统计意义，仅\\\\n    find-animal（+4.2）可能有真实信号。这进一步削弱 non-death 信号。'\\\\n  related_experiments:\\\\n  - C2 重心从 router 转向 labeling method：episode-level counterfactual labeling 绕开 per-step\\\\n    oracle circularity，本身是 methodological contribution\\\\n\\\\n    - 这样 plan-011 威胁降低：论文贡献不在于\\\\\\\"router 比 keywords 好\\\\\\\"，而在于\\\\\\\"counterfactual analysis 揭示\\\\n    death avoidance 机制 + 诊断框架\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n    评估：Reviewer 的结构性论证正确。Episode-level labels 确实无法教 step-level discrimination（D008\\\\n    98.2% tie rate → Plan C → 但 Plan C 的标注粒度太粗）。这是一个独立于预筛计数的结构性限制。\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n    决策：framing pivot 近确定执行。仍等预筛完成获取完整数据（论文需要），但 paper_framing.md 立即更新方向。实验计划不变——router\\\\n    SFT 仍做（负面结果有论文价值），plan-005/011/012 优先级提升。'\\\\n  related_experiments:\\\\n  - Several others like plan-005, plan-006, plan-008, plan-009, and plan-010 are still planned. The critical finding is that death avoidance drives roughly 90% of the routing signal, with a death avoidance factor of 90.41% and a Pearson correlation of 0.9698 at high statistical significance.

The DAF dual reporting fix in R6 is significant—they addressed the penalty-dependence issue by reporting both the DAF range (46-95%) and the co-equal headline metric (78.9%), so I need to verify these numbers are internally consistent.

GPU resources are idle right now, which is appropriate for the current paper writing and review phase. I should also verify whether the plan-004 negative result is honestly presented in the paper, and cross-check the framing claim about the 78.9% non-tie labels being driven by death asymmetry against the actual plan data.

But from plan-012, the non-tie labels total 95 (86 det-preferred plus 9 internal-preferred), and 70 out of 86 det-preferred cases are death asymmetry-driven, which is 81.4%—still not matching the 78.9% headline number. I'm working through the possible calculations to figure out which denominator produces that specific percentage.

I notice the ROADMAP mentions a DAF range of 46-95%, with the main DAF at 90.41% at the default penalty, so I should verify what drives that range.

\"notes\": \"Generation-based routing eval 推翻了此前 logit-level 的 success 结论。\\n\\n**Logit-level (Trainer eval, 训练时)**: accuracy=74.5%, balanced_acc=79.1%, internal_recall=84.9% — 看起来成功。\\n**Generation-based eval**: accuracy=53.3%, balanced_acc=41.5%, internal_recall=26.7% — 实际失败。\\n\\ninternal_recall=26.7% < 30% 判定线 → conclusion 改为 negative。\\n\\n三种 thinking 模式（thinking_on, thinking_off, thinking_on+strip）结果完全一致，说明 thinking mode 不是根因。\\n\\n与 ablation-b 对比: accuracy 53.3% vs 65.4%, balanced_acc 41.5% vs 59.3%, internal_recall 26.7% vs 51.5%。全面落后。\\n\\n与 plan-002-generative-v1 同样的问题模式：logit-level 指标与 generation-level 指标严重不一致。模型在 next-token prediction (loss) 层面学会了区分路由，但在自回归生成时无法可靠地输出 [SIMULATE] 前缀。\\n\\n根因推测：causal LM 的 generation 和 teacher-forcing eval 是不同的推理模式。训练时看到完整序列可以区分路由，但生成时第一个 token 的选择（是否输出 \\\"[\\\" 开始 [SIMULATE]）不够确定。\"}, \"plan-003-baseline-245eps\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-14T08:11:02.369Z\", 样本更少。\\n\\n**v2 的数据确实不同**（标签比例反转：internal 67% vs 10%，只有 4 个 task_type），但 v2 依然收敛了（eval_loss 0.49）。这说明数据差异导致训练行为不同，但不是致命问题。\\n\\n**建议下一步**：\\n- 用 ablation-b 的配置（upsample_minority=true, ```text
Now I have a thorough understanding of the project state. Let me synthesize my ROADMAP review.

Key findings:

**1. ROADMAP 更新合理性**
- R6 fresh review 3.25/5, all fixes documented and completed
- Critical path (R7→iterate→polish→submit by 5/24) is realistic for 10 days
- GPU budget appropriate: no experiments needed, both GPUs idle

**2. Data provenance for plan-012**
plan-001b-gpu0 failed, plan-001b-gpu0-v2 failed, plan-001b-gpu0-v3 failed. 

The 245 episodes came from multiple successful runs across different GPUs and configurations—plan-001b-gpu1 contributed 80 episodes, plan-001b-fnlt-v2 added 19 more for the find-non-living-thing task, and plan-001b-gpu1-freeze-chem captured the freeze and chemistry-mix scenarios. Several GPU0 attempts were killed, though some tasks like lifespan already had sufficient data from earlier runs, and various prescreening data was reused throughout.

Now looking at plan-004, which tests explicit versus implicit routing approaches. The explicit router version shows strong logit-level performance at 79.1% balanced accuracy but completely falls apart at generation level with only 41.5%. The implicit router does better at generation (58.1±0.8%) but that's barely above random. The end-to-end evaluation confirms this is worse than the baseline deterministic approach, which actually supports the paper's narrative about why this direction doesn't work.

The review score pattern is interesting—fresh reviews consistently come in 0.25-0.5 points lower than continue reviews, and continue reviews tend to improve by 0.5 points each round. If R7 is a continue review building on R6's 3.25 baseline, I'd expect it to land around 3.5-3.75 if it addresses the main issues.

Now looking at the experiment coverage, I've completed the core experiments needed: prescreening, data collection across 245 episodes and 11 tasks, baselines, ablations, the rule-based baseline that showed structural failure, the death decomposition analysis that confirmed the key finding, and labeling quality checks. The router SFT wasn't done formally, but the ablation and generative experiments effectively cover that ground. Routing pattern analysis would strengthen the story with another figure, but it's not essential for the short paper format.

I'm noticing a few tracking issues though. Plan-001b shows as failed in the experiment registry, but the actual data came from multiple runs afterward—it's a documentation problem rather than a scientific one. And the report is outdated, still referencing an earlier session number.

There's also an important methodological finding from plan-004-v5 about logit-level metrics diverging from generation-level metrics that should be addressed if the paper reports router training results. The death-asymmetry calculation from plan-013 checks out at 78.9% when I verify the numbers across preferred and internal conditions.

Looking at the DAF range from plan-012-penalty-sensitivity, the values span from 46.4% to 94.5%, which aligns with the ROADMAP's stated range of 46-95%. The episode and task counts also match exactly what's documented. Overall, the ROADMAP appears well-constructed and the project is ready for submission—the core finding about death avoidance holding at ~90% has strong statistical backing, the negative router results actually reinforce the main conclusion, and the rule-based baseline's structural failures are honestly presented.

There are a few minor issues worth noting: the plan-001b tracking points to a failed run, Report.md could use updating, and some planned experiments from plan-005 never ran but were effectively replaced by other work. These feel like info-level concerns with maybe a warning about the experiment tracking.

But I should double-check the review timeline. The ROADMAP mentions R6 got a 3.25/5 score (borderline accept, below the 3.5 target), and R7 is scheduled for 5/15 to continue the review. Since the R6 fixes have already been implemented, R7 should be verifying those corrections. Looking at the fresh review pattern—2.25, 3.0, 3.25—there's a steady upward trend, which is encouraging.

The continue reviews show 2.75, 3.25, 3.5, also trending up. If R7 builds on the R6 score of 3.25, I'd expect to land around 3.5-3.75 based on that pattern, which would hit the target. There are also a couple of cosmetic float warnings in the compilation that should get cleaned up before final submission.

D006\\n- id: D008\\n  date: 2026-05-13 17:09 UTC\\n  summary: 1-step labeling 失败 (tie rate 98.2%), plan-001b 必须改为 episode-terminal counterfactual\\n    labeling\\n  rationale: 'MVP tie-rate 分析: 2880/2894 步 (98.2%) 两种模式 1-step score delta = 0 (稀疏\\n    reward)。非 tie 步几乎全是 catastrophic gap (death penalty)。1-step oracle labeling 下\\n    router 只能学到\\\"避死\\\"——等价于 always-det on death-risk tasks。必须改为 episode-terminal counterfactual\\n    labeling: 每步 fork 环境，两条模式各自继续到 episode 结束，以 final episode score 差异作为 label。Phase\\n    0 已验证 forking 可行 (p95 latency 2.37s)。GPU 成本显著增加——需要 Agent 估算 action divergence\\n    rate 和实际工时。plan-001a (pre-screening) 和 plan-007 (boil rerun) 不受影响，继续运行。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: mvp-p1-gpu0-vllm\\n    role: source\\n    result: tie rate data from episodes.jsonl\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D007\\n- id: D009\\n  date: 2026-05-13 17:15 UTC\\n  summary: 'Counterfactual labeling 分 pilot + 全量两阶段执行。Pilot: 3 tasks × 10 ep × K=10\\n    on GPU1。Non-tie rate > 30% 则全量 K=10，< 15% 则 pivot。'\\n  rationale: Action divergence rate 73.1% 确认 per-step routing 有足够 action space，但 1-step\\n    tie rate 98.2% 排除了 1-step labeling。Episode-terminal counterfactual labeling (fork\\n    at diverge + K-step continuation) 是必要方案。Full K=20 成本 78h 双卡太高。Pilot 先验证 K=10 的\\n    label quality (non-tie rate)，再决定全量策略。Pilot 选 melt/change-state/mix-paint（MVP 确认有信号），10\\n    ep each（~7-8h 单卡）。如果 K=10 non-tie rate < 15%，per-step routing 可能是 wrong granularity，需要\\n    pivot。\\n  related_experiments: []\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D008\\n- id: D010\\n  date: 2026-05-13 18:13 UTC\\n  summary: C2 重构为 \\\"failure trajectory predictor\\\"，paper_framing v3 更新 Plan C 定位\\n  rationale: Reviewer 分析 Plan C (episode-level labeling) 对论文 story 的三个影响：(1) Router\\n    学到的是 trajectory-level risk prediction 而非 step-level routing，C2 应重构为 \\\"failure trajectory\\n    predictor + diagnostic probe\\\"；(2) Plan C 完全绕开 Dream worst case 的 oracle circularity\\n    攻击（不用 per-step tie-breaking，直接用 episode outcome ground truth）；(3) 不做 class weighting，label\\n    不平衡反映真实分布。Story 一致性提升：safety-aware framing ↔ episode-level labeling ↔ trajectory\\n    risk prediction 三者天然匹配。C1 \\\"selective > unconditional\\\" 的关键指标变为 simulator invocation\\n    ratio（用更少的 simulator calls 达到接近 always-det 效果）。\\n  related_experiments: []\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D010\\n- id: D011\\n  date: 2026-05-13 19:09 UTC\\n  summary: Framing 调整：det 优势 = 纯 death avoidance，non-death 时 internal 略优。C1 机制明确为\\n    \\\"safety-efficiency tradeoff\\\"，router 学习 death-risk detection 而非泛泛路由。Labeling 策略维持\\n    episode-level 但加 death-aware 后处理。\\n  rationale: 13 tasks (8 prescreening + 5 MVP) 一致显示：no-death Δ(D-I) = -4.5 ~ -0.4，det\\n    的全部 30-60 分优势来自 internal 的 41-49% death rate vs det 的 5-12% death rate。这意味着：(1)\\n    always-det 不是最优（safe steps 有性能代价），selective routing 有理论基础；(2) router 的核心任务是 death-risk\\n    detection；(3) plan-001b episode-level labels 将以 death episodes 为主要信号源，non-death\\n    episodes 提供少量 internal labels；(4) plan-012 death decomposition 升为论文核心证据。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - 若 internal-preferred < 3/25（<12%）→ 执行 framing pivot：C1 从 \\\"selective > unconditional\\\"\\n    降级为 \\\"det >> internal, 主要机制是 death avoidance\\\"；C2 调整为 \\\"router 学习 death-risk trajectory\\n    prediction\\\"；plan-012 (death decomposition) 升为核心贡献；plan-011 (rule-based baseline)\\n    变关键（learned vs heuristic）\\n\\n    - 若 internal-preferred ≥ 3/25（≥12%）→ 保持现有 framing，plan-002 router SFT 使用 class-weighted\\n    loss 应对不平衡\\n\\n\\n    不选的方案：(b) 人为增加 internal episodes → p-hacking 风险；(c) 加 MATH track → 12 天 DDL 下风险太大\\n\\n\\n    另外 Reviewer 指出 genuine-det 分类中 find-living-thing（+0.25）和 freeze（+2.0）在 n=5 下无统计意义，仅\\n    find-animal（+4.2）可能有真实信号。这进一步削弱 non-death 信号。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - C2 重心从 router 转向 labeling method：episode-level counterfactual labeling 绕开 per-step\\n    oracle circularity，本身是 methodological contribution\\n\\n    - 这样 plan-011 威胁降低：论文贡献不在于\\\"router 比 keywords 好\\\"，而在于\\\"counterfactual analysis 揭示\\n    death avoidance 机制 + 诊断框架\\\"\\n\\n\\n    评估：Reviewer 的结构性论证正确。Episode-level labels 确实无法教 step-level discrimination（D008\\n    98.2% tie rate → Plan C → 但 Plan C 的标注粒度太粗）。这是一个独立于预筛计数的结构性限制。\\n\\n\\n    决策：framing pivot 近确定执行。仍等预筛完成获取完整数据（论文需要），但 paper_framing.md 立即更新方向。实验计划不变——router\\n    SFT 仍做（负面结果有论文价值），plan-005/011/012 优先级提升。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - Deterministic external computation (environment simulators, code interpreters) eliminates these failures — but what exactly does routing learn? We introduce episode-level counterfactual analysis (per-step oracle labeling degenerates to 97.4% ties in sparse-reward settings) and discover that **death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal** on ScienceWorld (Δ_full=+29.60 [A], Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], DAF=90.41% [A]; Pearson r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A] across 11 task types / 245 episodes [A]; source: plan-012-full). Episode-level label distribution: det-preferred 86 / internal-preferred 9 / no-preference 150 (out of 245 episodes) [A]. Death rates: internal 42.9% / deterministic 16.3% [A]. Non-death score deltas are small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84 [A], 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). Key exception: find-non-living-thing (n=40, zero deaths, Δ_full=+9.58, all non-death performance difference) [A]. Pearson r=0.9698 (p=8.12e-07) [A] between per-task death rate difference and Δ_full across 11 task types [A]. The value of deterministic computation in interactive environments is concentrated in catastrophic failure prevention, not positive task guidance.\\n\\n**C2 (Episode-level counterfactual labeling as diagnostic methodology)**: Per-step oracle labeling degenerates in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld). Episode-level counterfactual analysis — running complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies — overcomes this circularity and reveals the death-avoidance mechanism. The resulting labels train a lightweight router (Qwen3-1.7B) that serves as a **diagnostic probe** for LLM failure modes. [Hypothesis, pending plan-005]: the router may learn within-task-type early warning signals of catastrophic failure from early observations, beyond simple task-type classification.\\n\\n**D013 note**: C1 no longer claims \\\"selective > unconditional.\\\" Router training (plan-002/003) proceeds — if router ≈ always-det, that IS the finding; comparison with rule-based baseline (plan-011) quantifies whether learned routing adds value over keyword heuristics.\\n\\n## Positioning vs Tool-Use Literature (Dream-driven addition)\\n\\n**Critical gap identified by all 3 Dream tiers**: The core pipeline (counterfactual labels → SFT small classifier → selective invocation) is standard in tool-use learning. Must position against:\\n- **Toolformer** (Schick et al. 2024): Self-supervised tool-use learning via API call insertion. SimGate differs: hindsight labels from environment forking (not self-supervised), safety-aware framing (not general tool selection).\\n- **ART** (Paranjape et al. 2023): Automated tool selection via LLM reasoning. SimGate: dedicated lightweight router, not LLM-as-router.\\n- **Chameleon** (Lu et al. 2024): LLM-as-planner routing to diverse tools. SimGate: single modality (deterministic computation), explicit small router.\\n- **ToolkenGPT** (Hao et al. 2024): Token-level tool triggers. SimGate: step-level routing with counterfactual labels.\\n\\n**Our differentiation**: (1) Episode-level counterfactual labeling — overcomes per-step oracle circularity in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate), providing ground-truth episode outcomes rather than tie-break artifacts. (2) Empirical finding: death avoidance accounts for ~90% of routing signal [A] — quantifies WHERE and WHY tool invocation helps (catastrophic failure prevention, not positive guidance). (3) Diagnostic framework — counterfactual analysis + router as probe reveals systematic LLM failure modes across task categories. (4) The contribution is the finding + methodology, not the router architecture — this distinguishes from \\\"we built a better tool selector\\\" papers.\\n\\n**Paper must include a dedicated Related Work section** (not just intro paragraphs). Minimum coverage: tool-use learning (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon), adaptive computation (CogRouter, ARES, Mixture-of-Depths), world model routing (ITP-R, Dyna-Think), agent frameworks (ReAct).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: LLM agents hallucinate state transitions on rule-governed environments (temperature physics, chemical reactions), leading to catastrophic failures. ITP-R routes to an approximate LLM world model but underperforms pure internal reasoning (62.58 vs CogRouter 84.6 on ScienceWorld). ReTool/AgentMath learn when to invoke code interpreters but use implicit routing entangled with the backbone. Tool-use learning methods (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon) learn general tool selection but don't specialize for catastrophic failure prevention or use counterfactual trajectory labeling.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We use episode-level counterfactual analysis to understand WHAT routing learns. Per-step oracle labeling degenerates (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld), so we run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode-level outcomes. Key finding: ~90% of routing signal is death avoidance [A] — deterministic computation's value is almost entirely concentrated in preventing catastrophic failures (-100 penalties). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84, 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). A router trained on these labels becomes a diagnostic probe for LLM failure modes, but (per D013) is structurally limited to task-level risk classification under episode-level labels.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"Episode-level counterfactual analysis across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes) [A] reveals that death avoidance accounts for 90.41% of routing signal [A] between internal LLM reasoning and deterministic computation (Δ_full=+29.60, Δ_nd=+2.84 [A]; r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A]). Episode-level counterfactual analysis — running complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies — overcomes this circularity and reveals the death-avoidance mechanism. The resulting labels train a lightweight router (Qwen3-1.7B) that serves as a **diagnostic probe** for LLM failure modes. Must position against:\\\\n- **Toolformer** (Schick et al. 2024): Self-supervised tool-use learning via API call insertion. SimGate differs: hindsight labels from environment forking (not self-supervised), safety-aware framing (not general tool selection).\\\\n- **ART** (Paranjape et al. 2023): Automated tool selection via LLM reasoning. SimGate: dedicated lightweight router, not LLM-as-router.\\\\n- **Chameleon** (Lu et al. 2024): LLM-as-planner routing to diverse tools. SimGate: single modality (deterministic computation), explicit small router.\\\\n- **ToolkenGPT** (Hao et al. 2024): Token-level tool triggers. SimGate: step-level routing with counterfactual labels.\\\\n\\\\n**Our differentiation**: (1) Episode-level counterfactual labeling — overcomes per-step oracle circularity in sparse-reward environments (97.4% tie rate), providing ground-truth episode outcomes rather than tie-break artifacts. Minimum coverage: tool-use learning (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon), adaptive computation (CogRouter, ARES, Mixture-of-Depths), world model routing (ITP-R, Dyna-Think), agent frameworks (ReAct).\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: LLM agents hallucinate state transitions on rule-governed environments (temperature physics, chemical reactions), leading to catastrophic failures. ITP-R routes to an approximate LLM world model but underperforms pure internal reasoning (62.58 vs CogRouter 84.6 on ScienceWorld). ReTool/AgentMath learn when to invoke code interpreters but use implicit routing entangled with the backbone. Tool-use learning methods (Toolformer, ART, Chameleon) learn general tool selection but don't specialize for catastrophic failure prevention or use counterfactual trajectory labeling.\\\\n\\\\n- **Our framing**: We use episode-level counterfactual analysis to understand WHAT routing learns. Per-step oracle labeling degenerates (97.4% tie rate on ScienceWorld), so we run complete episodes under pure-internal and pure-deterministic strategies, comparing episode-level outcomes. Key finding: ~90% of routing signal is death avoidance [A] — deterministic computation's value is almost entirely concentrated in preventing catastrophic failures (-100 penalties). Non-death performance difference is small but non-negligible (Δ_nd=+2.84, 9.6% of Δ_full [A]). A router trained on these labels becomes a diagnostic probe for LLM failure modes, but (per D013) is structurally limited to task-level risk classification under episode-level labels.\\\\n\\\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\\\\\"Episode-level counterfactual analysis across 11 ScienceWorld task types (245 episodes) [A] reveals that death avoidance accounts for 90.41% of routing signal [A] between internal LLM reasoning and deterministic computation (Δ_full=+29.60, Δ_nd=+2.84 [A]; r=0.9698, p=8.12e-07 [A]). Router-as-diagnostic-probe reveals systematic LLM failure modes. Explicit vs implicit ablation quantifies the gap.\\\\n\\\\n2. **\\\\\\\"Only one benchmark (ScienceWorld)\\\\\\\"**\\\\n   - Mitigation: Short paper format justifies focused evaluation. ScienceWorld uniquely tests rule-governed dynamics with catastrophic penalties. Discuss MATH/API extension as future work.\\\\n\\\\n3. **\\\\\\\"Comparison unfair — CogRouter uses RL (CoPO), you use SFT\\\\\\\"**\\\\n   - Mitigation: Our primary comparison is SimGate vs ablated baselines (always-internal, always-deterministic, random, heuristic) — all same training budget. ```text
Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze this major change:

**Key change: Short paper (4p, Findings) → Long paper (8p, Main Conference)**

This is a very significant upgrade with only 10 days to deadline. Let me assess:

**1. What's new in the ROADMAP:**
- Target changed from "EMNLP 2026 ARR short paper (4 pages)" to "EMNLP Main Conference Long Paper (8 pages)"
- New experiments needed:
  - Multi-model validation (second LLM backbone on ScienceWorld) - P0
  - MATH full track (500 problems) - P0
- Writing expansion:
  - Full formalism, labeling pipeline pseudocode, DAF derivation
  - Independent Related Work section
  - Independent Discussion section
  - Deep analysis (temporal patterns, per-task deep dive, failure mode taxonomy)

**2. Critical concerns:**

**A. MATH track has been PROVEN NEGATIVE (D045/D046)**
The ROADMAP lists "MATH full track — 500 problems" as P0, but earlier documents explicitly ruled this out. D045 and D046 show the MATH pilot already failed—internal performance significantly outpaced the code interpreter baseline, which contradicts what we'd need to demonstrate.

The signal is inverted compared to ScienceWorld: code interpreter doesn't provide the routing mechanism we expected. If we expand to 500 problems, we're just amplifying a negative result. For the long paper, MATH would need to be framed as a deliberate contrast case showing where the approach breaks down, not as validation of the same finding.

**B. Multi-model validation is new and untested**
Running a second LLM backbone introduces variables we haven't controlled for yet.

Setting up vLLM with a different model, collecting counterfactual episodes across modalities, and analyzing death patterns in 2 days is aggressive. The real risk is if death avoidance doesn't replicate across models—that would undermine the paper's core narrative.

**C. paper_framing.md NOT updated**
The framing document still references the 4-page short paper format and EMNLP 2026, but we're now writing a long paper. This mismatch needs to be resolved before we start drafting.

**D. D046 revoked the multi-environment router approach**
The roadmap is essentially returning to multi-environment validation, but now framed as an analysis paper rather than a method paper. The question is what MATH is supposed to prove—since it has no death penalty, there's no death avoidance signal to find there. If we're using it as a negative control to confirm death is the mechanism, that's scientifically valid, but the roadmap description about "500 problems, code interpreter as deterministic oracle" doesn't clearly articulate that logic.

**E. The timeline is punishing**
Ten days to run new multi-model experiments, validate on 500 MATH problems, integrate results, do deep analysis, iterate on reviews, and polish for submission is extremely tight for a long paper that needs substantially more content and a restructured narrative. The short paper required many rounds to reach 3.25/5, so starting fresh with reviewers on a longer submission will be even more demanding.

**F. No documented decision for this upgrade**
There's no D050 entry explaining the rationale for moving to a long paper—the latest decision is D049 about the R3 weak accept.

**G. GPU constraints are real**
Running ~245 episodes across two modalities on a new model plus 500 MATH problems will be resource-intensive given the crash history we've seen. Splitting across separate GPUs helps but doesn't eliminate the risk.

**3. Critical issues to surface:**

The MATH track framing contradicts earlier decisions (D045/D046/D047)—I need to clarify whether it's meant as positive validation or negative control. The paper_framing.md hasn't been updated for the long paper format, and there's no formal decision record documenting this upgrade path.

I'm reconsidering my approach here. The user explicitly requested this upgrade, so my role is to assess whether the plan is sound, not override their venue choice. The MATH framing contradiction is genuinely critical because the data suggests something different from what the experiment assumes. The stale documentation is a warning, and the scientific risks warrant flagging. Looking at the ROADMAP, it mentions expanding MATH from 105 to 500 problems with the code interpreter as a deterministic oracle, but it doesn't resolve whether this is positive validation or negative contrast. I should write the review with these specific concerns in mind.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have everything I need for my analysis. Let me draft the review.

Key findings:

**1. CRITICAL: MATH track framing contradiction**
ROADMAP lists "MATH 500-problem full evaluation" as P0 for the long paper. But:
- D045: MATH pilot shows internal 80% > code interpreter 72%, signal REVERSED
- D046: "撤销 PIVOT（D041/D042），回归分析论文。MATH 不扩全量"
- D047: "MATH-200-fixed 不恢复。105 题足够"


The experiment plan still has plan-009 marked as P2 claiming it "demonstrates generalization beyond environment simulators," yet the data shows the opposite — running 500 MATH problems will likely produce negative results where internal performance exceeds code interpreter performance. This could actually be valuable evidence for the paper, but the ROADMAP needs to explicitly frame this expectation before committing GPU resources, otherwise it'll look like wasted effort and create confusion.

**2. CRITICAL: paper_framing.md not updated**
paper_framing.md still references outdated content that doesn't align with the current experimental direction.

**3. WARNING: Multi-model validation is a scientific risk**
Testing a second LLM on ScienceWorld could strengthen the paper's generality claims, but there's no pilot data yet for other models on this pipeline, and different models exhibit different death rates and reasoning patterns — if the death avoidance pattern doesn't replicate across models, it weakens the core claim, though replication would be strong evidence. The real risk isn't the experiment itself but whether there's enough time to handle ambiguous results before the deadline.

**4. WARNING: experiment_plan.yaml not updated for new experiments**
The roadmap marks "Multi-model validation" and "MATH 500" as P0 priorities, but they're not registered in experiment_plan.yaml yet — no formal specs, no GPU budget estimates, nothing to track them.

**5. WARNING: No formal decision record**
Upgrading from a short paper to a long paper is the biggest strategic shift in the project, but there's no decision document capturing the rationale, risks, and fallback plan.

**6. INFO: Timeline assessment**
The critical path looks tight but doable — running experiments through mid-May, integrating results, then reviewing and polishing before submission.

**7. INFO: plan-001b provenance fixed**
The experiment plan now has proper documentation for how plan-001b was assembled across multiple runs, and the registry ID has been updated to plan-012-full, which resolves the earlier concern.

Now I'm drafting the review.
```
